# Reach

> English | [日本語](./README-ja.md)

Whether sockat reaches a long path, by which route, and if not, why, for each combination of conditions. Built for the design of [DR-0017](../../docs/decisions/DR-0017-resolve-through-persistent-base.md) (Japanese; base B reused, resolve's spellings first, no fork) and the [SPEC](../../docs/SPEC.md). The numbers are estimates from the lengths of the spellings.

- Page that filters by the conditions you choose: [index.html](index.html?lang=en) (without JS, read this README)
- Source of truth: [model.json](model.json). This README (English and Japanese) and `data/` are generated by [gen.py](gen.py) (`just reach`); do not edit them by hand. `just reach-check` catches drift
- The fork-cwd technique and where it reaches: [fork-cwd](../fork-cwd.md)

## How rows are folded

- One table per operation and OS. A row is a combination of conditions; the columns are the conditions and the result
- Every combination is computed. Rows with the same result (reached, route, spelling, reason, basis) that differ in the value of one condition only are merged into one row, repeatedly until nothing merges
- The file name, and the shorter of the original and the relative path, are split into bands at the lengths where some route's spelling starts or stops fitting. Within a band the result does not change (gen.py checks both ends of each band)
- A condition that is "any" in every row of a table gets no column; it is listed above the table under "Conditions with no effect"
- Base lengths are typical values (the bases table under "Conditions"). The directory is assumed deep enough that the path is over the standard library length; the whole path's length has no effect up to PATH_MAX
- Languages are split only where a route they cannot build (lacks) changes the result

## Estimates and measurements

- A cell whose basis is "estimate" is only computed from the spelling formulas and typical values in model.json
- A "measured" cell names the conformance scenes ([conformance/cases.json](../../conformance/cases.json)) where each language's implementation took the same route as computed (versions and date in model.json's evidence). Applied only to rows of connect routes that need no writing and no thread (at-direct, proc-direct, relative, widened), and only for scenes whose conditions are all known
- Inputs that were measured: which OSes' bind follows a symlink ([findings](../../docs/findings/2026-11-19-bind-through-file-symlink.md), Japanese), that another uid cannot traverse a 0711 directory under a 0700 parent (the measurements in the Context of DR-0017), and that docker's seccomp refuses `unshare` ([conformance.md](../../docs/design/conformance.md), Japanese)

Assumptions of the estimates:

| Value | Bytes | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `D` | 10 | D's name (random, 10 alphanumerics; DR-0017) |
| `E` | 2 | the name of resolve's E, a base-36 counter within the process (DR-0017). 1 character for up to 36 and 2 for up to 1296; estimated at 2 characters |
| `Er` | 10 | the name of a per-call E (thread-cwd, at and the default connect's link-file) (random, 10 alphanumerics; DR-0017) |
| `Bname` | 11 | the length of B's name sockat-<uid>. Estimated with a 4-digit uid (e.g. 1000); macOS's 501 is 1 shorter, a larger uid is longer by as much |
| `Bname_runtime` | 6 | the length of B's name sockat under $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR |
| `pid` | 7 | the digits of <pid> in procB. 7 for Linux's pid_max 4194304 (a smaller pid reaches a longer file name by as much) |
| `fd` | 2 | the digits of the fd number (2) |
| `name_max` | 255 | the file name limit (NAME_MAX). The file name bands in the tables go up to it |
| `widened` | 253 | the path length that fits a widened sockaddr on Apple OSes (widened in the SPEC) |

## Conditions

| Condition | Operations it affects | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| OS | all |  |
| Language | all |  |
| File name (bytes) | all | the band edges are computed from the spelling length formula of each route |
| Shorter of the original and the relative path (bytes) | default connect | the spelling of relative and widened. The relative path is built from the cwd ("Building the relative path" in SPEC-routes) |
| Thread cwd usable | default bind<br>bind_ex<br>default connect | Linux: no seccomp filter and unshare succeeds; Apple OSes: pthread_fchdir_np exists |
| /proc usable | all |  |
| bind follows a symlink | default bind<br>bind_ex | a condition only on OSes where it is not measured (the default bind and bind_ex tell at run time); on measured OSes the OS decides. resolve_for_bind uses link-file only on the measured OSes (macOS, NetBSD) |
| /tmp | default bind<br>bind_ex<br>resolve_for_bind<br>default connect<br>resolve_for_connect | the first base for B |
| $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR | default bind<br>bind_ex<br>resolve_for_bind<br>default connect<br>resolve_for_connect | the second base for B |
| $TMPDIR | default bind<br>bind_ex<br>resolve_for_bind<br>default connect<br>resolve_for_connect | the third base for B |
| Socket's directory writable | default connect<br>resolve_for_connect | the fourth base for B. bind creates the socket file, so it is assumed writable |
| Options.base | bind_ex<br>resolve_for_bind<br>default connect<br>resolve_for_connect | the path of B itself. When given, the automatic bases are not used. Among the connect operations only the connect_ex variants take it, as the base for B. In a bind operation with usable_cross_uid, EPERM unless the path and the base itself give other x. Errors of the other checks (a missing parent etc.) are not in the tables |
| PrivateTmp (either side has /tmp isolated) | default bind<br>bind_ex<br>resolve_for_bind | systemd's PrivateTmp etc. Affects only whether the peer can follow the spelling |
| flock(1) available | default bind<br>bind_ex<br>resolve_for_bind<br>resolve_for_connect | the flock command of util-linux or busybox is on PATH. Only sockat-ts uses it to lock D (the other languages call flock(2) directly, so it has no effect on them). Without it, the routes that use D are not usable |
| Options.usable_cross_uid (peer with another uid) | bind_ex<br>resolve_for_bind | given, talking to a peer with another uid. Affects which B is chosen (only bases whose path and B itself give other x), not using procB, and whether the peer can follow the spelling |

How B is chosen ("How B is made" in [SPEC-routes](../../docs/SPEC-routes.md)):

- Without `Options.base`, the bases are looked at in the order `/tmp` → `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` → `$TMPDIR` → the socket's directory. Misses known without making anything (the base is absent; with `usable_cross_uid`, no x for other) are skipped, and the spelling length is estimated at the first remaining base. If it does not fit, the walk stops there and does not move to the next base. If B cannot be made after passing the estimate (not writable etc.), the same is done at the next base. `Options.base` is estimated before it is made too. A B in `/tmp`, `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` or `$TMPDIR` is one per process; a B in the socket's directory is one per directory. The tables are computed for the first call of a process that holds no B yet
- The spelling is procB (when `/proc` is usable and it fits), then B's realpath. When B is in the socket's directory, the realpath spelling is longer than the original path, so only procB
- With `usable_cross_uid`, only bases in the same order whose path and B itself give other x are used for B, and procB is not used. This B is held apart from the usual B and shared with it when it is the same directory. If none, or it does not fit, thread-cwd / at
- The E of thread-cwd and at is made in D of the usual B (the first base that can be made). B does not appear in the spelling, so the estimate does not apply. For bind the socket's directory is writable, so B can always be made

Bases (typical values. The length is the realpath's bytes; B's realpath is the base + `/` + B's name. With `Options.base`, the given place is B. A base that is not writable still has a length, as it is used for the estimate before making B):

| Base | OS | Value | Length | Length of B's realpath | Another uid can traverse | Under /tmp or /var/tmp |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| /tmp | Linux / Android<br>macOS<br>iOS and the other Apple OSes<br>FreeBSD<br>NetBSD<br>OpenBSD<br>illumos<br>other Unix | writable (1777) | 4, 12 | 16, 24 | ○ | ○ |
| /tmp | Linux / Android<br>macOS<br>iOS and the other Apple OSes<br>FreeBSD<br>NetBSD<br>OpenBSD<br>illumos<br>other Unix | not writable (still used for the estimate before making B) | 4, 12 | 16, 24 | ○ | ○ |
| $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR | Linux / Android | /run/user/<uid> (14, 0700) | 14 | 21 | × | × |
| $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR | Linux / Android | /run/user/<uid> not writable (14; still used for the estimate before making B) | 14 | 21 | × | × |
| $TMPDIR | Linux / Android<br>FreeBSD<br>NetBSD<br>OpenBSD<br>illumos<br>other Unix | /var/tmp (8, 1777) | 8 | 20 | ○ | ○ |
| $TMPDIR | Linux / Android<br>FreeBSD<br>NetBSD<br>OpenBSD<br>illumos<br>other Unix | deep (113, under 0700) | 113 | 125 | × | × |
| $TMPDIR | macOS<br>iOS and the other Apple OSes | default (/private/var/folders/…/T, 56, 0700) | 56 | 68 | × | × |
| $TMPDIR | macOS<br>iOS and the other Apple OSes | deep (113, under 0700) | 113 | 125 | × | × |
| the socket's directory | Linux / Android<br>macOS<br>iOS and the other Apple OSes<br>FreeBSD<br>NetBSD<br>OpenBSD<br>illumos<br>other Unix | writable | — | — | — | — |
| Options.base | Linux / Android<br>macOS<br>iOS and the other Apple OSes<br>FreeBSD<br>NetBSD<br>OpenBSD<br>illumos<br>other Unix | /tmp/app-<uid>/sockat (20, under 0700) | 20 | 20 | × | ○ |
| Options.base | Linux / Android<br>macOS<br>iOS and the other Apple OSes<br>FreeBSD<br>NetBSD<br>OpenBSD<br>illumos<br>other Unix | deep (113, under 0700) | 113 | 113 | × | × |

Spelling symbols: `<F>` is the file name, `<rel>` the shorter of the original and the relative path, `<B>` B's realpath, anything else a value from "Assumptions of the estimates". sockaddr is the length that fits a sockaddr (107 on Linux / illumos, 103 elsewhere). `(B: …)` in the route column of the tables is the B that was used, named by the values of the diagnostic `base` (`given` for the B of `Options.base`)

## Summary: reach by file name length

Every combination of conditions in the tables, summarized per file name band. "Depends" is a band reached or not depending on the thread cwd, `/proc`, the bases, Options, the relative path's length and so on. "Reached in a typical environment" assumes: the thread cwd and /proc are usable, the bases are the OS defaults and writable, no Options are given, PrivateTmp is not involved, and on Linux flock(1) is available. For the relative path's length, and for whether bind follows a symlink on OSes where it is not measured, a band counts as reached only when every value reaches

## File name length reached in every environment (lower bound)

The tables above are estimated with typical digits (4-digit uid, 7-digit pid, 2-digit fd, 2-character E). This section uses the worst digits. A file name up to this length (bytes) reaches in every environment. If a test or a guide fixes a length, use this lower bound (the tables' values with typical digits are longer)

| Value | Worst digits | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `pid` | 7 | digits of procB's pid. Linux's pid_max upper bound 4194304 (the largest pid is 4194303) |
| `fd` | 7 | digits of B's fd. Linux's fs.nr_open default 1048576 (the largest fd is 1048575) |
| `uid` | 10 | digits of the uid in B's name. uid_t is 32 bits and the largest valid uid is 4294967294 |
| `E` | 2 | characters of resolve's E name. Up to 1296 E in one process's resolve |

Premise: B has the default name `sockat-<uid>` and is in `/tmp`. On Linux the figures come from the route spellings (`<B>/<D>/<E>/<F>` etc. in the routes table) with the worst digits.

| OS | Operation | Condition | Lower bound |
|---|---|---|---|
| Linux | bind | thread cwd not usable (docker's default seccomp etc.) | 70 |
| Linux | bind | thread cwd usable | 96 |
| Linux | connect | B can be made in `/tmp` (link-file) | any file name |
| Linux | connect | B cannot be made, proc-direct only | 85 |
| macOS | bind | B is in `/tmp` (link-file) | 255 (NAME_MAX) |
| macOS | connect | B is in `/tmp` (link-file) | 255 (NAME_MAX) |
| macOS | bind | B cannot be made in `/tmp` and `$TMPDIR` is deep (thread-cwd) | 92 |
| macOS | connect | B cannot be made in `/tmp` and `$TMPDIR` is deep (thread-cwd-direct) | 103 |

- For a Linux bind without the thread cwd, when B is in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (it cannot be created in `/tmp`, including when someone else created `/tmp/sockat-<uid>` first), the floor of the real-path spelling is 65 bytes (2-character E). Where `/proc` is usable the procB spelling is tried too, so the reach is the longer of the two (procB is 68 with the worst digits above). Without `/proc` this value is the floor

## Tables

### default bind

the same order as bind_ex, without Options. The spelling is kept (E stays until the process exits and goes with D in the OS's cleanup)

#### default bind / Linux / Android

| Language | File name (bytes) | Thread cwd usable | /proc usable | $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR | $TMPDIR | /tmp | PrivateTmp (either side has /tmp isolated) | flock(1) available | Reached | Route | Spelling | Peer can follow the spelling | Why not reached | With fork-cwd | Basis |

### default connect

connect, connect_nonblocking and the connect_ex variants (rows with Options.base given are the connect_ex variants only). Creates no D; link-file goes through an E right under B as B/E and removes E right after connect. A DGRAM connect and send_to take the same routes (whether send_to avoids relative is open in the SPEC)

### resolve_for_connect

returns the spelling B/D/E (link-file), kept until cleanup and reused for connects to the same path
